Moscow Wants Say in International Caspian Oil
Deal

LD1010170395 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1623 GMT 10 Oct 95

(By Arseny Mironov]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, October 10
(ITAR-TASS) — Russia urges for a “northern,” or
Russia-traversing route of pumping Caspian oil to
Europe, Russian Foreign Ministry senior spokesman
Grigoriy Karasin told ITAR-TASS today.

The deal under which oil from Caspian offshore deposits
is to be transported to Europe was signed in London by
Azerbaijan and an international oil consortium.

The oil deal is of extreme importance for Russia,
Karasin said.

"We still believe that the resources of the Caspian
Sea belong to all Caspian (littoral) states, and their
development should be carried out jointly on a long-
term basis,” he said.

He called the “northern” transportation route “most
profitable,” adding that this is not challenged by parties
to the deal, but the issue involves interests of business
circles and oil companies of other states, including
multinationals.

The international oil consortium decided to use both
Russia- and Georgia-proposed routes, which spells that
crude oil from the Caspian deposits is to be transported
via Russia.

Negotiations are in progress with Georgia on using a
pipeline in its territory for moving petroleum products
at a phase when the three Caspian deposits reach target
outputs.

A high-placed official of the Russian oil company
Transneft, an active negotiator of future transportation
routes, told ITAR-TASS that delegations of Azerbai-
jan’s State Oil Company and Azerbaijan’s International
Operational Company, which is the working body of
the Western consortium, are to come to Moscow next
week.

Russian and Azerbaijani delegations will draft a contract
to renovate a Baku-Groznyy-Novorossiisk oil pipeline
for pumping the oil.

The Transneft spokesman said Russia will be prepared
to transport an annual 500,000 tonnes of the Caspian oil
in the second half of 1996 and two times that amount
two years later, building it to an annual five million
tonnes by 2002.

Kounineft, Egypt Oil Ministry Reach Concession
Deal

LD1010210695 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1958 GMT 10 Oct 95

[By Georgiy Shelenkov]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] CAIRO, October 10 (ITAR-
TASS) — A concession deal was signed here today by
the Russian oil company Komineft and the Egyptian Oil
Ministry.

The contract leases to Komineft [Komi Oil concern]
a 22,000 square kilometres area east of Cairo for 35
years, with a maximum of eight years granted for oil
prospecting.

Komineft is to invest 3.5 million dollars within the first
two years, with more funding of the prospecting project
to be made thereafter in several disbursements.

Should oil be struck in this area, the Russian company
will get 17.5 per cent of oil, on terms that the daily
output is above 50,000 barrels.

Egyptian Oil Minister Hamdi al-Banbi, who is the
signatory to the contract, stressed the good image and
technical equipment of the Russian company. He voiced
hope for success of the project.

The Russian signatory, Komineft’s director-general An-
drey Yakomov said the negotiations with the Egyptian
side were not easy.

He said in an interview with ITAR-TASS that Komineft
takes serious risks.

However, analysis holds out a promise that what oil the
company extracts in the area will cover expenditure on
the project and bring profit, Yakimov said.

{[FBIS Translated Text] The compromise reached by
the international consortium can to some extent be
interpreted as a Russian viciory. The crux of the matter

RUSSIAN INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 33

is that the United States could not push through a
unilateral decision on transporting oil through Georgia
alone, although U.S. companies in the consortium have
slightly less than 44 percent against 10 percent for
Russia’s LUKoil.

The prospect is far from hopeless for Russia, even if one
suggests that for well-known reasons pumping oil across
Chechnya to Novorossiysk would scarcely be possible
for God knows how many decades.

The reason is that the option calling for oil trans-
portation across Georgia should not be characterized
as exclusively "Turkish." Russia still has formidable
geopolitical influence on the hotbeds of potential armed
conflicts (Georgia versus Abkhazia, Azerbaijan versus
Nagorno-Karabakh). Meanwhile, the “Turkish route"
passes right across the territories of ethnic standoff.

The proof of Russia’s considerable clout in the region
are Georgian offers to the Russian Government to buy
a 49-percent share in the "Turkish" oil pipeline. This
percentage is extremely high, although certain sources
assert that Russia demands 51 percent, that is, absolute
commercial control over the oil pipeline.

Still, the consortium’s current decisions are made rather
for reconnaissance purposes and will not have a decisive
impact on the development of Azerbaijani fields. For the
time being it is a matter of not more than 2 million
tonnes of so-called early oil that will be produced
annually on the Chirag-1 platform. Azerbaijani oil
production will not reach its peak for 10 years.

Shumeyko: ‘No Reasons’ for Haste in NATO
Expansion

LD1010200595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1942 GMT 10 Oct 95

[By Lyudmila Yermakova and Gennadiy Temnenkov]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] BONN, October 10 (ITAR-
TASS) — Russia and Germany have different attitude to
NATO's eastward expansion. This has been confirmed
again by a conversation on Tuesday between Speaker
of the Russian Federation Council Vladimir Shumeyko
and German Foreign Minister Klaus Kinkel.

“There are no reasons in the world at present that would
warrant haste about NATO's expansion," Shumeyko
told ITAR-TASS after the meeting. "We do not want
the process of NATO’s expansion beginning at all.
Germany plays a huge role in this."

It is necessary to consider, without taking any steps,
what happened after the disruption of the bi-polar
system in the world and after the breakup of the USSR,
he said. “Thank God, nobody threatens anyone now, and
the question of security is not acute.”

"NATO’s expansion has been decided on," Kinkel
believes. The point of the matter is who should be
admitted into NATO and in what timeframe. A survey
of prospects for NATO’s expansion was submitted last
week. "In that survey Russia, too, is shown how these
processes may look,” he said.

The West has been maintaining a dialogue with Rus-
Sia after it signed the Partnership for Peace programme.
This dialogue must lead to the establishment of spe-
cial partnership with Russia, to its inclusion into the
structure of European security. Germany also supports
Russia’s wish to enter the Council of Europe. "No new
obstacles should be placed in this way,” the minister
said.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

Shumeyko believes that there are military and eco-
nomic rather than political differences in the debate
on NATO’s expansion. Russia which experiences eco-
nomic difficulties at present and is short of cash for the
investment into the military-to-civilian conversion is ab-
solutely not interested in NATO's expansion and in the
entry into it of former members of the East European
bloc that used to be the main buyers of Russian military
equipment and spares, Shumeyko said.

Russia needs the war industry now not as a means of
arms manufacture in the struggle for the division of the
world but as a base for preserving high technologies.
This is important for the success of the economic reform
whose objective is the raising of the living standards.

Germany.”

with the German side on the problems which were
discussed."

He said Russian and Germany were on the common
ground over some 70 per cent of issues.

As for Germany’s possible aid to Russia, Shumeyko
said Russia needs none.

“We need investment to maintain our reforms and a
serious understanding of our problems,” he said, adding
that a strong Russia is in interests of Germany as much
as Russia wants a strong Germany.

(FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 10 Oct (ITAR-TASS)
— Today in Moscow, an official spokesman of the

RUSSIAN INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 35

Russian Foreign Ministry presented a view on the
detailed plan for the transfer of power to the legally
elected civilian government in Nigeria. The plan was set
out in the program statement by General Sani Abacha,
the head of the Nigerian military regime, in connection
with the 35th anniversary of country’s independence.

Grigoriy Karasin, the director of the information and
press department of the Russian Federation Foreign
Ministry, said that the transitional program covers a
three-year period. A new constitution will have to be ap-
proved, the presidential system will have to be changed,
and a multiparty system will have to be introduced. By
the end of this year, the ban on political activities will
be lifted. It has also been announced that sentences im-
posed on the participants in the conspiracy against the
military regime in March, including Olusegun Obasanjo,
the former Nigerian president, will be alleviated. Re-
cently world public opinion, including Russia, spoke
out actively in the latter’s defense.

When the plan for the democratization of Nigeria, which
was made public, is analyzed, one can see on the
whole its positive nature, the official spokesman of
the Foreign Ministry stressed. Political goals which are
vitally important for Nigeria have been set and specific
decisions and dates for their implementation have been
adopted.

G. Karasin said that, according to the international
response already heard, the timescale appears too long.
Incidentally, Moscow believes that the transition of
such a gigantic country as Nigeria to democracy is a
complex process. The fact that the military regime has
publicly undertaken to bring this process to a successful
conclusion is a really important fact.

the Federation Council.”

Ilyushin said.

Yeltsin Signs Law on Referendums

LD1010152295 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1402 GMT 10 Oct 95

{(FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, October 10
(ITAR-TASS) — President Yeltsin signed today the
federal constitutional law "On a referendum in the
Russian Federation," adopted by the State Duma on
July 7, 1995, and approved by the Federation Council
on October 3, 1995, a spokesman for the press service
of the president told Tass.

The law says that a referendum, together with free
elections, is the supreme and direct manifestation of
people’s power and is held on the basis of universal,
equal and direct expression of their will by secret ballot.
All Russian citizens above 18 years of age, with the
exception of those who were recognized as incapable
by a court ruling or are kept in prison, have the right to
take part in a referendum.

Under the law, problems dealing with the changing of
the status of subjects of the Russian Federation, the
termination or prolongation of the term of office of the
president, the Federation Council or the State Duma,
the introduction and lifting of federal taxes and dues,
as well as some other problems, may be put up for a
referendum. Referendums cannot be held in conditions
of martial law or a state of emergency.

Referendums can be initiated by Russian citizens of the
Constitutional Assembly.

The federal constitutional law becomes effective from
the day of its promulgation, says a report of the presi-
dent’s press service. After the adoption of the constitu-
tional law, a number of legal adts adopted before, specif-
ically the law "On a referendum in RSFSR" dated Octo-
ber 16, 1990, and the resolution of the RSFSR Supreme
Soviet "On the procedure for putting into effect the RS-
FSR Law ‘On a referendum in RSFSR’", dated October
16, 1990, become invalid.

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

Yeltsin Aide Interviewed on Legal Reform Needs

MM0610120395 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 6 Oct 95 pl

[Interview with Russian presidential aide Mikhail Kras-
nov by Dmitriy Orlov; place and date not given: "The
Legislators Are Trying To Harness a Young Horse to
an Old Wagon, Creating New Laws Without Changing
the Legal System at the Same Time. Russian Federation
Presidential Aide Mikhail Krasnov Claims in an Exclu-
sive ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI Interview That the Solu-
tion of This Acute Problem Is Possible Only by Means
of Legal Reform" — first paragraph is ROSSIYSKIYE
VESTI introduction]

(FBIS Translated Text] A three-day scientific and prac-
tical conference devoted to drawing up a blueprint for
legal reform in Russia, with the participation of repre-
sentatives of the Russian Federation Presidential Staff,
the Federal Assembly, the Government of Russia, the le-
gal system, and leading scientific institutions, has been
held outside Moscow.

[Orlov] Mikhail Aleksandrovich, which structures will
bear the heaviest burden in the implementation of legal
reform?

(Krasnov] All state institutions which come in contact
with the law must implement legal reform. Namely,
the Presidential Staff's State Law Administration, the
Ministry of Justice, and the federal parliament. And,
of course, the reform must be implemented under the
auspices of the president.

Many people have misunderstood the thrust of legal
reform. The point is not so much to come up with a
host of new laws as to replace one legal system with
another. The system which should prevail is a system
based on political and economic freedom.

As far as the methods of implementing the transforma-
tions are concerned, it is primarily a matter of clear-
ing up and systematizing legislation. The piles of de-
partmental acts which are at odds with the Constitu-
tion and the laws generate problems in the practical
application of the law. The link between the laws and
the provision made for them in terms of finance and
resources is weak. The constitutional provision which
states that there must be a government ruling [zak-
lyucheniye] on budget-related draft laws is not being
implemented in full: Deputies treat it in a purely per-
functory way, merely taking account of negative gov-
ernment rulings.

Moreover, there is no system of accountability for
specific individuals for failure to execute a particular
normative act, nor any system of monitoring institutions,
and there is a lack of legislative guarantees for the

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 37

activity of professional experts. In short, there is no
system, whereas I see our salvation as lying precisely
in having a system.

[Orlov] The notion that Russian legislation is incom-
plete is a familiar one. What, in your view, are the most
substantial gaps in our laws?

{Krasnov] I have already mentioned the absence of
a system of accountability. Moreover, there is no
normative basis for monitoring the activity of the
Federation components. In general, in the defense of the
public interest (as distinct from the defense of private
interest) there is an enormous gap. For example, the
law on the general principles governing the organization
of power in the Federation components has still not
been adopted, and this poses a substantial threat to the
functioning of its institutions. In France, for example,
observance of legality in the departements is ensured by
prefects; we have no such institution.

[Orlov] What, from the legal standpoint, could stymie
the State Duma elections?

[Krasnov] Back in the spring I said that the president
did not intend to oppose the elections to the lower
chamber of parliament. No matter how bad the election
law may be, elections are the only real lever of control
over power. Abandoning them would be tantamount to
plunging headiong back into Soviet power, in the worst
sense of that term. Thus virtually nothing, from the legal
standpoint, can block the holding of elections.

The fact that you could get a situation as a result of
the failings of the law whereby a faction which wins
20 to 30 percent of the votes will obtain two-thirds of
the seats in parliament is another matter. A deposit to
be paid for participants in the election would therefore
be a more acceptable option. But, I repeat, the election
legislation has already been adopted.

As far as the possible criminalization of deputies is
concerned, here something must be done with regard to
the rules governing deputies’ immunity. A whole system
of measures is required, in particular more careful
vetting of prospective deputies by the law enforcement
agencies. And this is no return to totalitarianism:
Because the danger of the criminalization of power is
very great today, the fight against this tendency has to
become a priority. I fervently support Sergey Shakhray’s
initiative on the mandatory declaration of prospective
deputies’ incomes.

{Orlov} Recently many people have been saying that
the possible confederal union of the Russian Federation
and Belarus will lead to the abrogation or postponement
of the presidential election. How do you regard such
views?

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

38 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

[Krasnov] A confederal system does not abrogate the
existence of the organs of power of the constituent
parts of the confederation. The process of creating a
confederal union is not a process which can be achieved
overnight. Nor can a referendum on union be held right
away, even leaving aside the fact that it will be very
difficult to carry out changes to the fundamental laws
of the states in question even if a referendum produces
a pro-union result.

[Orlov] The Federation components have differ-
ing views on the head of state’s edict advising the
rescheduling of the elections to organs of state power
and local self-government. What can you say on this
score?

{[Krasnov] Refraining from long commentary here, I
would simply say that I am not enraptured with this
proposal which has been put to the president.

FBIS-SOV-95-196

east" devastated half of Russia and rendered it lifeless.

Therefore, no matter how much NATO Secretary Gen-

no longer threatens us, we know from our historical ex-
perience that the West has always feared a strong Rus-

represented easy prey.

The present situation is at first sight completely differ-
ent. Russia is weak, but the West is cooperating with it
with all its might, even when Russia does not want to
cooperate. But the question is, why has it been deemed
necessary to surround Russia again, and draw former
USSR republics and East and Central European coun-

the Soviet years we did not recall this often, but it is
after all a fact that it was not just volunteer forma-
tions from France, Belgium, Italy, Romania, Hungary,
Croatia, Slovakia, and many other countries, but also
national-fascists from the former USSR countries that
frequently fought within the SS troops on the Eastern
front, and indeed under the banners which have now
become their state banners. Do we have the right in this
case to act on the basis of the principle "it’s best to bury
the past"!? After all we might be buried ourselves.

The West is bearing in mind of course that despite their
present cowed situation, the Russians, who are paying
with their own dignity and purse for the “democratic”
experiments of the nouveaux riches from the former and
present nomenklaturas, have nevertheless not lost their
historical memory and will not give their motherland
away for bribes and handouts. Furthermore, they could
forget their differences and unite in the name of saving
Russia not on an ideological basis but on a purely
patriotic basis. Then the overt and covert quislings of
the West in Russia could lose the levers of power.
That is if, of course, Russia is given the possibility of
voting in a truly democratic manner and the results of
the people’s will are not dishonestly rearranged. It is
well known how attentively the West is tracking the
preparations for the imminent elections in the Russian
Federation and how many professional advisers and
election campaign specialists it has already sent there.
Clearly they are not serving the left-wing forces or the
patriotic forces. Obviously NATO strategists on both
sides of the Atlantic Ocean will not be sparing in their

expenditure on maintaining the "democratic" status quo

Duma Member Claims Government Falsified Crop
Figures

LD0610133995 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1153 GMT 6 Oct 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 6 (INTER-
FAX) — Communist member of the State Duma and
the Russian Academy of Agricultural Sciences Viktor
Shevelukha suggests that the federal government ex-
plain why it falsified grain production reports and tor-
pedoed this year’s plans for grain production.

In a memo distributed among Duma members Fri-
day, Shevelukha accused the Deputy Prime-Minister
in charge of the agricultural complex Aleksandr Zav-
eryukha of “repeatedly telling citizens that at least 78
million tonnes of grain would be harvested this year"
while experts assessed the condition of the sown areas

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 39

only to decide that Russia would harvest no more than
65 to 67 million tonnes of grain. Three times running
Shevelukha briefed the government on the experts’ fore-
casts for 1995, the memo said. (Russia produced 81.3
million tonnes of grain in 1994 and 116.7 million tonnes
in 1990.)

Shevelukha shared his view of the situation with Prime-
Minister Chernomyrdin, Deputy Prime-Minister Zav-
eryukha, Food and Farming Minister Nazarchuk and the
Duma. The realization of suggestions voiced by farming
experts would have enabled Russia to harvest up to 85
million tonnes of grain but “the government refused to
heed and meet those suggestions,” Shevelukha said.

At present, Food and Farming Minister Aleksandr
Nazarchuk admits that 65 to 67 million tonnes of grain
were to be harvested this year. He explains the poor
crops by a drought.

Shevelukha blames the poor crops on “something that
has been forcibly imposed on the agricultural complex
under the name of a reform." The so-called reform
brought the Russian agricultural complex to ruin, he
said.

Media Issues

Daily Outlines Provisions of Yeltsin Broadcasting
Edict

MM1110102795 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 10 Oct 95 p2

{Article by Nikita Dubnov: “Our Comments on Presi-
dential Edict. Sword of Damocles No Longer Hanging
Over Television"]

{FBIS Translated Text] What constituted the sword of
Damocles were financial difficulties and the television
companies’ constant nonpayment of bills to the commu-
nications industry, which in turn threatened a broadcast-
ing blackout and in places carried out its threats. The
situation became intolerable, especially on the eve of the
elections. Quite a few other problems connected with
broadcasting also built up. Then Russian Federation
President B. Yeltsin signed an Edict "On the Improve-
ment of Television and Radio Broadcasting in the Rus-
sian Federation" 6 October. As it says, its aim is to make
state support for the electronic mass media more effec-
tive. Three radio [as published] broadcasting companies
— Russian Public Television, the All-Russia State Tele-
vision and Radio Broadcasting Company ("Ostankino”),
and “Peterburg-5 Channel" and three radio stations —
“Radio-1," "Mayak," and "Yunost-Youth Channel” are
receiving all-Russia status. This concept is being intro-
duced for the first time and means that these broadcast-
ing organizations, whose signals reach more than half
of the Russian Federation’s components, receive a se-
ries of benefits. In particular, they are exempted from
the obligatory payment of part of their foreign currency
earnings on the condition that this capital is channeled
into the development of their materials and technical
base. All state television and radio broadcasting orga-
nizations — both all-Russia and regional ones — will
pay for the services of the communications industry ac-
cording to tariffs stipulated for budget-funded organiza-
tions. Regional state television and radio companies are
moreover being given a status equal to that of cultural
institutions, which also gives them considerable bene-
fits, including tax concessions.

The government is being given the task of completing
work on the draft federal program for the development
of television and radio broadcasting in the Russian Fed-
eration. The program’s main areas should be the es-
tablishment of regional networks for the broadcasting
of programs using federal and local budget money, the

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

attraction of foreign investment to bring on stream the
new Program Production Center at Shabolovka which is
as yet unfinished, construct a station in Klin for the re-
ception and broadcasting of programs, and to reconstruct
the Space Communications Center in Petushki. The gov-
ernment should also ensure that treaties are concluded
as quickly as possible with CIS countries and Lithuania,
Latvia, and Estonia on the procedure and conditions for
broadcasting all-Russia television and radio broadcast-
ing organizations’ programs on their territories.

Listeners to “Orfeya" need no longer worry about their
favorite radio station: It and a number of broadcastiag
Organizations are receiving the status of state institu-
tions. But with regard to "Mayak,” "Yunost," and the
"Peterburg-5 Channel" company, the Edict orders exam-
ination of the possibility of their being transformed into
joint-stock companies with 51 percent of their shares in
federal ownership.

The Edict approved the Russian Federation Govern-
ment’s proposal to abolish the Russian State Television
and Radio Company ("Ostankino"). This will permit the
protracted structural changes on the first channel to be
completed.

{[FBIS Translated Text] Boris Yeltsin appears to have
gotten down to the arms trade in earnest. The other
day he signed the edict "On Military-Technological
Cooperation of the Russian Federation With Foreign
Countries" and a corresponding statute. In fact, the
president effectively moved to take control over any
decisionmaking regarding the export and import of
weapons, military hardware, and military-related works
and services.

From now on, cooperation with any countries will be
“established, suspended, stopped, or resumed" only on
the basis of Boris Yeltsin's edicts. The president’s main
arms trade adviser will be the Russian Federation State
Committee on Military-Technological Policy. It is this
agency that will submit to the president proposals on
military cooperation matters and will also draw up draft
laws on all problems relating to the arms trade.

In addition, the State Military-Technological Policy
Committee will devise together with the Foreign Min-
istry proposals on compiling lists of countries cooper-

ation with which should be banned or drastically re-
stricted.

Border Service Director Nikolayev Interviewed

MMI1110092995 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
10 Oct 95 pS

{Interview with General A. Nikolayev, director of the
Russian Federal Border Service, by Valeriy Yakov in
the general’s aircraft while flying abroad and in gen-
eral’s office in the Lubyanka; date not given: "Gen-
eral Nikolayev’s Bailiwick" — first two paragraphs are
IZVESTIYA introduction]

(FBIS Translated Excerpt] For almost two years now,
almost from the moment that A. Nikolayev, deputy
chief of the General Staff, a man then not well known
to the wider public, was appointed deputy minister
of security and commander of the Russian Federation
Border Troops, there has been no let-up in press interest
in this figure. In numerous forecasts he is destined now
for the post of defense minister, now for chief of a
reformed General Staff, then again, in the long term,
for the vice presidency...

Nikolayev has periodically denied these predictions and
occupied himself with setting up Russia’s new border
service. Where he has succeeded, and where has he not,
whether he intends to make a career in politics — about
this and much else I spoke to him on board plane during
one of his routine visits abroad; we also spoke for more
than two hours in the autumnal evening in his official
office in the Lubyanka...

[Yakov] Andrey Ivanovich, during your visits to "hot
spots,” you are invariably accompanied by a bodyguard.
You have strong young men at your side in Moscow as
well. Please tell me who they are — officials of the

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 41

Main Security Directorate or the president’s Security
Service?

[Nikolayev] We have our own security service which
safeguards our security during the leaders’ business
trips, especially on visits to the Tajik-Afghan border, the
Caucasus.... These are specially trained border guards
officers. We have never been guarded by Main Security
Directorate staffers.

[Yakov] That is to say, the fears characteristic of Krem-
lin functionaries — that Korzhakov’s or Barsukov’s
people could be listening in to their conversations —
are unreal for you?

{Nikolayev] I have never spoken with my colleagues in
this office about anything that should be hidden from
the services you mention, still less from the president.
Our position is well known — we are servants of the
state, and we do not propagate two opinions, one for
internal use and one for official consumption. And we
see the supervision which the Federal Security Service
[FSB] exercises over the border troops along with its
counterintelligence functions as perfectly normal. It is
essential. Just as collaboration with the MVD [Ministry
of Internal Affairs] and the foreign intelligence service
is essential.. Everything intersects at the border —
politics, economics, and security questions.

[Yakov] So the rumors that the new chief of the FSB
is beginning to make attempts to take back the border
troops under the wing of his own department have no
truth in them?

[Nikolayev] I have known Mikhail Ivanovich Barsukov
since I was appointed commander in chief of the border
troops. And not once has there been an occasion where
disagreements have arisen between us. I think that all
this talk is no more than rumors deliberately initiated
by those who would like to create some kind of tension
in our relations.

[Yakov] Right now, in the run-up to the elections, when
the most unexpected teams are beginning to be formed
and many people have to choose to whom to give their
preference, what choice are you making?

[Nikolayev] Let me say frankly — I do not have to
choose. I do my job, and for me, as a military man,
there are the Constitution and the duties of a serviceman,
defined by the law, which clearly prescribes our tasks
and treats the role of the supreme commander in chief.
If a serviceman wants to run for parliament, he may —
let him suspend his service and exercise his civil rights.
If he wants to get involved in politics or participate
in the activity of some bloc, there is nothing wrong in
this, only he must leave the service. I apply all this to
myself in full measure. Therefore, I can state absolutely

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

42 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

definitely — I will remain director of the Federal Border
Service.

[Yakov] At a certain Security Council Session, when
the question of dismissing the power ministers was dis-
cussed, you voted for Grachev’s’ dismissal. Information
from these sessions does not usually filter through, and
only the official version is circulated. Do you not think
that on this occasion a deliberate leak was allowed?

(Nikolayev] The results of the ballot became known
from those who failed to fulfill the Security Council’s
decision, according to which everything that takes place
in the council should remain privy to the session’s
participants. We know who spilled the beans on this
occasion.

As for the ballot, it is necessary to take account of
a certain subtlety in the Security Council’s standing
orders. Decisions are adopted by permanent members
of the Security Council; the rest have the right to
participate in the discussion, to express their opinion,
and defend it. But it is the president, the premier, the
secretary of the Security Council, and the chairmen
of the upper and lower houses of parliament who
vote on the final decision. However, in this case the
president put the question to the vote of all participants
in the Security Council in order to ascertain the general
opinion. And I voted, knowing full well that my vote
would decide nothing, but at the same time not wanting
to be hypocritical toward Pavel Sergeyevich [Grachev],
or hide my opinion from him. In my view, this is an
honest stance.

[Yakov] In numerous forecasts you are destined for
certain new posts. Six months ago I myself wrote that
you were the most serious contender for the post of
defense minister. Now that the reorganization of the
General Staff into a single coordinating organ for all
military-related departments, including the Ministry of
Defense, is an entirely real prospect, you are being
spoken of as a possible leader of this organ. What do
you yourself think about such an appointment?

[Nikolayev] All good military men have a fire rule —
do not fish for a job, but do not run away from it either.
That is to say, if you have been entrusted with a share
in a task — do it. And do not seek a more comfortable
position. Everything will come in its own good time if
you work properly.

[Yakov] But there is a certain vulnerability in this
attitude. It is as if you opt for a wait-and-see tactic from
the very start.

[Nikolayev] I consider it improper to propose myself for
any leading role. But why do you think that I do not have
specific suggestions, or that I do not voice them? As the

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

leader of one of the military departments and a member
of the Security Council, I have expounded my opinion,
including in written form, on military reform not simply
of the Army — that is, only an individual institution —
but of the state [ya svoye mneniye po povodu voyennoy

that the country’s leadership knows about it.

[Yakov] Two years ago, in your first interview with
IZVESTIYA, immediately after your appointment to the
post of commander in chief of the Border Troops, you
said that you would have to not simply reorganize the
Border Troops, but create a new Russian Border Troops
Service. Have you succeeded?

[Nikolayev] If I were to answer affirmatively, that would
be incorrect.

Next year, 1996, should be the last year of the “transi-
tional” period and the first year of the implementation
of the federal border program, which we elaborated and
presented to the country’s leadership 1 September and
which was approved the president. Moreover, | would
like to stress that this is not a departmental document,
but a program coordinating the efforts of all structures
working on the border — the customs, the MVD, the
FMS [Federal Migration Service], and the FSB.

We face the task of creating or restoring the system
for safeguarding the border and returning, figuratively
speaking, to the old borders, but this time in the frame-
work of interstate agreements with the CIS countries.
Such agreements on joint border security exist with
Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Ar-
menia, Georgia, and Belarus. We have coordination
structures with Uzbekistan for the organization of bor-
der security on the Amu-Darya river and have signed
an agreement on cooperation with Ukraine.

[Yakov] Those who follow the situation in the Federal
Border Service can see with the naked eye the dynamic
transformations, and it is perfectly clear that reform is
in full swing in your department. But how then are we
to understand, on the one hand, the successful reform,
and on the other — such tragic events as the shooting
on the island of Popova and the similar tragedy which
preceded it?

[Nikolayev] The Russian Federation president made a
decision to staff the Border Troops with the best cadres
as a matter of priority. But we are not receiving draftees
of this caliber. Year by year the number of mentally
disturbed people and people who are underweight by
up to 30-40 kg is growing in our country. The standard
of education of draftees has dropped dramatically. That
is not to mention the people with convictions or with

11 October 1995

police records who have appeared in the troops. And
after all, a border troops detachment is not simply a
workplace; it means living together and keeping house
together... In short, it is a typical joint household, where
everyone knows everything about everyone.

[Yakov] And have you considered the fact that you

(Nikolayev] Every day approximately 500-600 docu-

In addition, the situation is a simpleton, you yourself

themselves not for Nikolayev, but for the sake of the
state.

[Yakov] For all the obvious changes that you have

[Nikolayev] It’s an ordinary panel house built in 1954.
There is nothing special in it and I go their quite rarely
because I finish work late and start early. It is more
convenient to travel from my apartment.

Zherebtsov Comments on Army Manning Levels,
Fall Draft

MM1110090595 Moscow KRASNAYA ZVEZDA
in Russian 10 Oct 95 p 3

[Report by Oleg Falichev: “Draft-95: Who Will Serve
and for How Long?"}

(FBIS Translated Text] As is already known, the Rus-
sian Federation president’s Edict No. 971 of 27 Septem-
ber this year announced the next draft of citizens for
military service. It will take place from October through
December. Over 200,000 new recruits will come into
the troops and fleets. According to estimates by special-
ists of the General Staff Organization and Mobilization
Main Directorate, this will make it possible to signifi-
cantly improve the situation with regard to the Armed
Forces’ strength level. For the shortfall today stands at
over 300,000 (!) men.

But something else is more important. It is the change in
the very ideology of the draft — which we have arrived
at after several years of democratic euphoria. What do
I have in mind?

The point is that this fall the switch to two-year draft
service has been made, and a number of unsubstantiated
deferments, more than 20 of which had accumulated —
something that exists in no other country — have been
abolished.

"As a result, we used to succeed in drafting just 20-
22 percent of those on the books," Colonel General
Vyacheslav Zherebtsov, chief of the General Staff

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

Organization and Mobilization Main Directorate, said,
“whereas in the FRG this figure stands at 50 percent,
and in Italy up to 80 percent. This figure is even higher
in other countries, where the only people not drafted
are those who are unable for health reasons to fulfill the
responsibilities of service."

To what did this lead? Figuratively speaking, to the
gradual weakening of the Armed Forces. Many units had
sufficient forces only to guard themselves. Officer guard
details even appeared. There was a sharp drop in combat
readiness.... Today it seems that awareness of the entire
catastrophic nature of the situation has gradually started
to get through to politicians and deputies. Many of
them are starting to realize that the Army just must not
continue to be treated in this way.

There has also been a sobering up with regard to
contract service. The slogan “Onward to a professional
army!" did not, alas, reflect quite accurately the potential
of our society and economy. This task could not be
resolved hurriedly. As is known, the times of bare
slogans have now vanished into the past. The true
military professional today wants a worthy assessment
to be made of his difficult and sometimes deadly
dangerous work. For the time being our state is patching
the holes in the budget with difficulty and is crudely
violating laws which it drew up itself. As a result,
last year 15,000 contract servicemen left the Armed
Forces last year alone because of low pay. Therefore
in the immediate future we will be left with the mixed
principle for manning the Army and Navy.

All the aforesaid was the reason why the new law "On
Making Changes and Additions to the Russian Federa-
tion Law ‘On Military Service Obligation and Military
Service’" was drawn up. KRASNAYA ZVEZDA has
already written about the changes in the terms of ser-
vice resulting from it. But since the editorial office is
continuing to receive questions in this connection, let
us repeat the main points. So, who retains the right to a
deferment, and who will serve and for how long?

No students are drafted into the Army. Those who study
at a higher educational institution [VUZ], a secondary
educational institution, or a vocational and technical
school are not drafted until they have completed their
studies and received their diploma. Nor are persons who
continue their post-VUZ education, for example as a
postgraduate.

Pedagogues and physicians working in a rural locality
are not drafted. Nor are citizens who have two children
or one child under the age of three years, as well as a
child without a mother. Nor those who care for a family
member who is in need of outside assistance and who
is fully provided for by the state. There are a number

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

of other deferments too. For example, a youth whose
brother was killed or died while serving may not be
drafted.

The principle of drafting VUZ graduates has been
altered somewhat. They serve, as a rule, for one year as
privates or sergeants. But after the military department
they can also serve for two years, albeit as officers. It is
up to the VUZ graduate himself to decide which option
to prefer. He has to report his choice to the drafting
commission.

The law sets the term of service for all others at 24
months. This also applies to those who were drafted
previously for 18 months and who were to have been
discharged this fall. Demobilization is being deferred
for them. Only those who were drafted in the fall of
1993 will be discharged in the fall of 1995. And, of
course, citizens with higher education drafted in the fall
of 1994.

A special approach is taken to participants in the
combat operations in Chechnya. Col. Gen. Vyacheslav
Zherebtsov said that the minister of defense has signed
two directives in this connection in October. Their
essence is that servicemen must serve no more than
six months in areas of armed conflicts. They will finish
serving the two-year term established by law in other
places. The personnel serving in the Chechen Republic
will be replaced literally during November-December.
Taking the prescribed leave into account, the servicemen
who are in Chechnya will still be discharged earlier than
all the rest by decision of the minister of defense.

{By Anatoliy Yurkin]

([FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, October 10
(ITAR-TASS) — Russian missile forces fired an
inter-continental ballistic rocket today in progress of
their command and staff exercises.

The exercises, which were conducted between October
4 and 10, drilled control of Russian strategic nuclear
forces.

ITAR-TASS was told in the press centre of missile
forces that the RS-12M Topol rocket blasted off from
the testing ground of the Plesetsk space port.

Missile force commander Colonel-General Igor
Sergeyev told ITAR-TASS that the rocket hit the target
with exquisite precision.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 45

Sergeyev said the exercises were to check the level of
combat readiness of duty personnel and missiles of the
strategic nuclear forces, and technical reliability of the
Topol booster carrying the RS-12M rocket.

All tasks of the exercises have been successfully ful-
filled, Sergeyev said.

MVD General Reports No Theft of Nuclear
Weapons Materials

LD1010140795 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1312 GMT 10 Oct 95

["No Military Nuclear Materials Stolen in Russia" —
INTERFAX headline}

[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 10 (INTER-
FAX) — General Andrey Terekhov of the Russian Inte-
rior Ministry [MVD] on Tuesday told a news conference
at the Interior Ministry that no cases of theft of radioac-
tive materials for making nuclear weapons have been
registered in Russia.

According to him, there are no organized mafia struc-
tures in Russia which specialize in stealing radioac-
tive materials. The general said that the investigation
of criminal cases shows that sources of ionizing radi-
ation which are used in insignificant quantities in dif-
ferent technical devices, in anti-fire transducers, in other
equipment and installations are the main objects of theft.
The general said that these radioactive substances by
their physical characteristics cannot be used for making
nuclear weapons.

He also said that the system of registration and protec-
tion of special materials existing at defense and nuclear-
energy enterprises, excludes the possible theft of ura-
nium and other components of nuclear weapons.

According to the Interior Ministry, in 1993 there were
detected 27 cases of illegal handling of radioactive
substances. In 1994 56 crimes were disclosed out of
which substances were stolen in 16 cases. There have
been nine cases of theft of radioactive materials over
nine months of 1995.

According to Interior Ministry’s spokesmen, the large
portion of thefts are made from Russian Nuclear Energy
Ministry facilities. As a rule, thefts are made by
personnel working on the sites, with 90% made by
ordinary workers.

Interior Ministry’s experts believe that the hullabaloo
around allegedly high prices for radioactive substances
at the black market is one of the reasons for the theft.

At the same time, a Nuclear Energy Ministry spokesman
has said that his department sells radioactive materials

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

46 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

for reasonable prices and there is no sense in stealing
them and gaining profit from their re-sale.

The media campaign on the alleged “Russian connec-
tion" concerning radioactive substances found in the
West is described by experts as an attempt by some
“Western countries to contrive reasons for penetrating
defense enterprises and taking control of their produc-
tion.”

The spokesman for the Interior Troops Commander,
General Sergey Korolev said that in 1994-95, the
Ministry sent an additional 6,000 servicemen and many
pieces of armored technology to better protect the
nuclear sites. He said that due to the difficult crime
situation in the country, interior troops focus their main
efforts on the safer protection of nuclear sites for them
to be invulnerable for terrorists.

against and four abstained.

Defense Committee Wants Military Budget
Increased

LD1010190095 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1658 GMT 10 Oct 95

([FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, Oct 10 (INTER-
FAX) — The State Duma Defense Committee decided
at a closed session Monday night that the 1996 draft
budget appropriates too little for defense and so should
be rejected, committee chairman Sergey Yushenkov,
Russia’s Choice, told INTERFAX Tuesday.

According to Yushenkov, defense spending has to
be increased by at least 30 trillion rubles. The draft
provides for nearly 80 trillion.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

Yushenkov explained that committee experts were pre-
suming that the size of the armed forces will remain
unchanged, even though he is convinced that the army
should be cut from 1.5 million to 1 million as should
other armed services such as the border guards, ministry
for Emergencies units etc.

Yushenkov admitted that reductions in armed forces
also result in increased spending because compensation
payments to officers and other expenses. He said that
direct payments could be replaced by state bonds or
allocation of landlots.

Further on Zadornov Budget, Bank Crisis
Comments

MMI1110091595 Moscow TRUD in Russian
10 Oct 95 p2

[Report by Igor Ostrovskiy: "Where Has the Money
Gone? Parliament Criticizes Government Draft Budget
for 1996")

{[FBIS Translated Text] “For the first time since the
reforms began the draft budget provides for a reduction
of 5 percent in the population’s real incomes. I am sure
no deputy would vote for such a draft.”

That was how Mikhail Zadornov, chairman of the State
Duma Budget, Taxes, Banks, and Finance Committee,
began his press conference held on the first day of the
parliamentary hearings on the government draft budget.

According to M. Zadornov, the draft not merely re-
peats, but aggravates the shortcomings of the current
year’s budget. It is unrealistic in revenue terms. The
deputies do not like its structure, that is, the reduction
of appropriations for the social sphere and investments.
The new budget, like the old one, enables the govern-
ment to spend money without controls and accountabil-
ity. M. Zadornov regards as uncontrolled expenditure
the money spent on Chechnya and also the credit of
4.5 trillion rubles (R] to certain enterprises and territo-
ries, which is not provided for in the budget. The par-
liamentarian is concerned about government officials’
unwarranted optimism. Many of them consider the Au-
gust banking crisis to have been successfully overcome,
whereas, according to M. Zadornov, it is only just be-
ginning. Many, even the completely reliable banks, are
experiencing a shortage of spare funds. This is resulting
in the flight of capital from the economy, it means that
enterprises are being refused credit, and payments are
being delayed. The "domino effect" could come iato op-
eration at any moment after the bankruptcy of any bank,
and this could lead to a payments crisis and the threat
of the total collapse of the economy. The deputy is also
averse to the policy of the Central Bank and the govern-
ment, which have attracted a group of court banks. He

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

believes that the right to manage budget funds should go
to all banks that have won that right on as competitive
basis and that are making a contribution to the treasury
for the right to use budget funds.

Zadornov believes that the situation in the banking
sphere is aggravated by the fact that several months
have gone by and the president has not signed the laws
adopted by both chambers of the Federal Assembly "On
Banks and Banking Activities" and "On The Securities
Market." Any further delay means the banks are in
danger of bankruptcy and of losing private investors
tens of millions of rubles.

“The government first of all has to answer the question:
“Where has the money gone?" M. Zadornov said,
concluding the press conference. "Finance Minister
V. Panskov said that the revenue part of the budget
will be implemented by 120 percent. Moreover budget
spheres are 80 percent funded and the military-industrial
complex is 50 percent funded. If the money exists,
where is it, what has it been spent on?"

A conciliation commission has been set up comprising
representatives of the government and both chambers of
the Federal Assembly. Whether the country enters the
new year with an approved budget depends on its work.

Duma’s Burkov on ‘Prohibitive’ State Property
Law

MK1110064995 Moscow NEZAVISIMAYA GAZETA
in Russian 10 Oct 95 p2

{Report by Tatyana Romanenkova under the “Privatiza-
tion” rubric: "One Cannot Make Much Money by Sales.
Selling Shares in Russia’s Key Enterprises for a Song
Is Impermissible"]

(FBIS Translated Text] Sergey Burkov, chairman of the
State Duma’s Committee on Property, Privatization, and
Economic Activity, called the presidential edict “On the
Procedure for Making Decisions on the Management
and Disposal of Federally-Owned Shares” fairly dubious
in legal terms.

Upon issuing the edict the president takes over man-
agement of the blocks of shares in enterprises that are
vital for the Russian economy. In Burkov’s view, al-
though this may make it possible somehow to supervise
the government’s policy of across-the-board privatiza-
tion ia strategically important sectors, the president may
not appropriate the management functions that, under
the Constitution, are still the Government's prerogative
— all the more so since he has no right to dispose of

state property.

Sergey Burkov thinks that only on the basis of laws
passed by the State Duma can one exercise effective

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 47

supervision over privatization in strategically important
sectors of Russian industry. This is precisely what
prompted the Duma committee chairman’s prompt and
fairly well-planned reaction to the aforesaid edict.

“At a time when market conditions are extremely
unfavorable, we should not allow the sale of Russia’s
key enterprises before year’s end,” Burkov said at
the State Duma session on 6 October, introducing the
federal law "On Disposing of Federally-Owned Shares
in Joint-Stock Companies Set Up in the Process of
Privatization."

The law is prohibitive in nature, which elicited doubts
from a number of deputies, but Sergey Burkov said that
the legislators had no option in the current situation. For
the sake of implementing the budget’s revenue part the
government is ready to sell for a song even enterprises
on which the strategic independence of our country
depends. Russian entrepreneurs and bankers clearly do
not have enough money to buy these blocks of shares,
and they are set to be bought by speculative foreign
Capital by proxy.

The law that the State Duma passed at Sergey Burkov’s
initiative in three readings at once prohibits such uncon-
trolled sales and is designed to help the key sectors of
Russian industry keep up their production potential.

Duma Aide Regrets Rejection of Lobbyism Bill

MM1010134595 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 7 Oct 95 pS

{Report by Olga Burkaleva under "Juridical Monitoring"
rubric: “Attempt To Narrow Scope for Corruption
Fails")

{[FBIS Translated Text] At its first session 4 October the
State Duma rejected the draft law "On Regulating Lob-
bying Activity in the Federal Organs of State Power,"
which was aimed at defining the mechanism governing
the interaction between society and the authorities at the
decisionmaking stage.

The Russian Federation Constitution guarantees citizens
the right to exercise their power directly (through elec-
tions and referenda), but also through the organs of state
power and self-government. They can appeal through
their representatives personally and send individual and
collective appeals to the state organs and organs of lo-
cal self-government. In addition to these methods of
interaction, there is also the path of putting pressure on
the representatives of power and establishing business
contacts with them. This is lobbying activity. The law
defines this as activity by individuals vis-a-vis the fed-
eral organ of state power aimed at exerting influence on
behalf of and in the interests of specific clients on the

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination|
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

48 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

elaboration, adoption, and implementation of legislative
acts and administrative decisions. A lobbyist is classed
as an independently acting citizen representing on the
basis of a contract or agreement the interests of a client
of the federal organs of state power. These clients can
be either Russian or foreign citizens and commercial or
noncommercial organizations, defending their own in-
dividual and group interests. However, foreign citizens
and organizations have the right to defend their inter-
ests in the federal organs of state power only through
Russian Federation citizens registered as lobbyists ac-
cording to the legally established procedure.

The draft law establishes the methods of lobbying
activity, the procedure for registering lobbyists, and the
mechanism for recording and monitoring their activity.

Restrictions on engaging in lobbyism are also stipulated.
In particular, deputies of the Russian Federation Fed-
eral Assembly and their assistants, officials of the Rus-
sian Federation legislative, executive, or judicial fed-
eral organs, elected or appointed officials of the Rus-
sian Federation components, and experts and consul-
tants appointed or invited by the Russian Federation
federal organs of state power cannot be lobbyists. It
was this stipulation, in the opinion of Vladimir Lep-
ekhin, deputy chairman of the Committee for the Af-
fairs of Public Organizations, who presented the draft
law, which was the cause of its rejection. Despite the
fact that deputies’ speeches were mainly positive, only
119 deputies voted for the draft law. Practically no one
voted against the draft law, but the remainder preferred
not even to abstain, but not to vote at all. Viadimir
Lepekhin told ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI’s correspondent
that this demonstrates that deputies are not interested
in adopting this law, since at the present time they can
engage in lobbying activity completely unchecked. The
law is designed to "illuminate" the lobbying process and
give it a civilized and accountable form. That is to say,
to narrow the scope for corruption. Today’s lobbying
machinery by which groups of citizens influence the
authorities is chaotic and blatantly fraught with corrup-
tion. In these conditions the stronger and more organized
structures that have connections and financial resources
have the advantage in interacting with the authorities.
At the same time groups of citizens and a whole series
of public movements have no access to the decision-
making process. In Lepekhin’s opinion, some lobbyist
clans now have far greater influence than political par-
ties and movements. The Duma’s failure to adopt this
draft law, the deputy believes, clearly proves the cor-
rectness of the accusation of lobbyism and corruption
leveled at some deputies.

And although this draft law was three years in the
making, although it has been discussed by experts and

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

representatives of the federal organs of state power,
by human rights campaigners, representatives of public
associations, and by entrepreneurs, and although it has
been presented at the CIS Interparliamentary Assembly,
and so forth, the process of its adoption will most likely
be just as difficult as that of laws relating to the sphere
of interaction between the authorities and society —
such as the laws on state service, on organized crime,
and others.

Deputy Regrets Human Rights Commissioner Law
Delay

MMI1010143295 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 7 Oct 95 p3

{Letter from Lyudmila Zavadskaya, State Duma deputy,
member of the "Women of Russia” faction, and chair-
man of the Subcommittee on Human Rights and Fed-
eral Legislation: "We Should Like Champions of Hu-
man Rights")

[FBIS Translated Text] ‘the draft law on a human rights
commissioner in the Russian Federation has been under
consideration in the State Duma for more than a year
now. It is one of 12 constitutional laws which will
lay the constitutional and legal foundations of Russian
statehood and supply the missing link in the mechanism
for protecting human rights and freedoms.

This institution has been introduced in more than 100
countries all over the world and has demonstrated
its effectiveness in restoring violated civil and human
rights.

The draft passed its first reading on 21 July 1994.
No one doubted thai it should be adopted as soon as
possible. The way people voted at the time speaks for
itself. Only one State Duma deputy voted against. The
basic concept and idea enshrined in the draft law were
approved by a qualified majority.

The idea is simple: A human rights commissioner will
restore and protect the violated rights of Russian citizens
and people in Russian territory.

Human rights in the Russian Federation are in a state of
Crisis. They are not only violated by officials, but also
by citizens themselves. The combat operations in the
Chechen Republic called into question the right to life
itself. Anyone mindful of this right would never take up
arms or set out to kill.

The human rights standards enshrined in the Russian
Federation Constitution are now only observed to the
extent that the current political situation and real eco-
nomic relations allow. We must be aware of one simple

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright own-:rs.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

rule: The violation of human rights leads to the disin-
tegration of Russian statehood and the breakdown of
stable relations in civil society.

We have inherited political, social, and economic prob-
lems from the Soviet Union in such numbers that no
state in the world could hope to resolve them without
tackling the key problem — human rights.

Society’s economic problems must not be ignored. They
concern everyone. But we must consider them from the
point of view of human rights.

There is one thing we must do — create the conditions
for the right to work. It is a right that must not be taken
away by the state itself. Only on the basis of the right
to work can we raise the question of social standards:
pensions, benefits, and payments.

Returning to the draft law on a human rights commis-
sioner, we should point out that its fate on the second
reading has been unfortunate. Four times now it has
been rejected by State Duma deputies for reasons un-
connected with its content.

I put it to you: Who will protect the rights of Russian
citizens?

Moscow’s Talks Position Unchanging

LD1110110295 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service
in Russian 1028 GMT 11 Oct 95

[By ITAR-TASS correspondent Sergey Ostanin]

{[FBIS Translated Text] Moscow, 11 Oct (ITAR-TASS)
— The position of the Russian side at the Groznyy talks
remains the same: There is no alternative to a peaceful
settlement of the situation in Chechnya, acting head of
the territorial administration Vladimir Zorin believes.
He told ITAR-TASS in a telephone conversation today
that the Russian side has not been officially informed
about a statement by the Chechen side to pull out of
the talks. It was reported that, following a decision
by Dzhokhar Dudayev, the Chechen side suspended
its participation in the talks on a peaceful settlement
in Chechnya and on implementation of the military
agreement signed in Groznyy on 30 July.

Zorin said that the mechanism for a settlement in Chech-
nya should remain the same: the implementation of ac-
cords on a package of military issues, the disarming of
illegal units, and the handover of weapons. Zorin be-
lieves that the tactics require some adjustment because
“terrorist acts in Chechnya, constant attacks on federal
forces, and the failure to follow the schedule for dis-
arming and the handover of weapons put the Russian
side in a difficult position.”

1413 GMT 11 Oct 95

[By Sergey Ostanin]}

[FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW October 11 (ITAR-
TASS) — Today’s statement of the Dudayev side

process and in the fulfilment of the package of military
agreements is a response to the statement of the Russian

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 51

The decision was made public at a special meeting of the
Chechen Committee for National Accord, the National
Revival Government and the staff of the Territorial
Branch of Federal Executive Authorities in Cl chnya.
The meeting was attended by Deputy Represent. .ive of
the Russian President in Chechnya Vladimir Rubanov.

At the same time, the federal and Chechen authorities
began to analyse a possibility of imposing a state
of emergency on Groznyy and some other areas of
Chechnya. Yeltsin formulated his stand on Tuesday. He
stressed at his meeting with Viktor Chernomyrdin that
“any steps, taken in the Chechen Republic, should be
careful and weli-considered." The president expressed
concern over the heightening of tension in the republic,
but said that the federal authorities had not exhausted
all possibilities of stabilising the situation.

“Talks on a peaceful settlement in Chechnya should go
on, but in the current situation some elements of the
talks should be changed,” Volskiy believes.

‘Toughest Measures’ Against Militants Urged

LD1010115695 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1022 GMT 10 Oct 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 10 (INTER-
FAX) — Russian Defense Minister Pavel Grachev sup-
ports the idea of a state of emergency in Chechnya.
In a Tuesday interview with INTERFAX he spoke
for tougher actions by federal troops against militants
throughout the republic.

He believes that the question of the immediate surrender
of arms by unlawful formations must be resolved fast.
Grachev said that in the event of disobedience they must
be destroyed.

In his opinion the assassination attempt on the comman-
der of federal forces in Chechnya Anatoliy Romanov
was organized by supporters of Dzhokhar Dudayev.

“This was a purposeful action. Now the Dudayev
leadership is trying to demonstrate that their units have
recovered from failure, they are organized, controllable
and adopt guerilla warfare methods. The main forms of
their operations are acts of sabotage, ambushes, night
attacks on federal troops and acts of terrorism,” the
minister said.

In his opinion hostilities in Chechnya will intensify in
late autumn. "Under the cover of fog and rain, with a
better knowledge of the locality militants will step up
guerilla warfare throughout Chechnya,” he said.

Grachev spoke for the toughest measures against mili-
tants.

Lobov Backs State of Emergency

LD1010212095 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1658 GMT 10 Oct 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text} GROZNYY, Oct 10 (INTER-
FAX) — The Russian president’s special envoy in
Chechnya, Oleg Lobov, has described as “very tense"
the situation in the republic following terrorist acts in
Groznyy. He made this statement on Tuesday in an
exclusive interview with INTERFAX right after the
meeting of federal command leaders in Khankala near
Groznyy.

"More active efforts should be made by all participants
in the current developments,” including by the republic’s
law-enforcement and state power bodies.

Asked by INTERFAX about what he thinks of the idea
to introduce a state of emergency, Lobov said that his

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

interpretation of the state of emergency differs from
that declared on Channel One of the Public Russian
Television, when it was said that he was against it.
"I am not against it, I am against haste in introducing
it, bypassing the procedures envisaged by the Russian
Constitution."

Lobov said that the republic’s national revival govern-
ment, the national accord committee, and the Groznyy
Mayor’s Office should be the first to have their say, be-
cause the issue involves their city. "Then they should
appeal to the Russian president," said Lobov.

Later, according to Lobov, "the president is to address
those who violate law and order, commit terrorist acts
and do not disarm."

“The president is to set the deadline, as envisaged by
the Constitution, before the introduction of a state of
emergency and, if his demands are not met, the president
is to introduce a state of emergency on the territory
where the demands are not being met," said Lobov.

According to Lobov, "the presidential decree together
with the request by the local authorities is to be handed
over to the Federation Council, which is to take a
decision within 72 hours."

{(FBIS Transcribed Text] GROZNYY, Oct 10 (INTER-

the process talked about so much in negotiations with
Romanov", he said.

He accused the rebels of delaying the implementation
of the military accord, adding that decommissioning
weapons would be a humanitarian act on the part of
pro-Dudyaev supporters.

Lobov described as unsubstantiated the rebels’ attempt
at accusing Russia of violating the accord.
