Meszaros Refutes Groznyy Disinvitation

LD1010180295 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1744 GMT 10 Oct 95

[By Ruslan Maisigov]

([FBIS Transcribed Text] GROZNYY, October 10
(ITAR-TASS) — There is no truth to press releases
of Chechen information chief Moviadi Udugov, who
alleged that the federal command in Chechnya formally
proposed to the Groznyy mission of the Organisation
for Security and Cooperation in Europe (OSCE) to
move out of the Chechen capital, the mission’s leader
Sandor Meszaros told ITAR-TASS.

He said the OSCE mission’s officials did not attend
the Tuesday meeting of the joint Russian-Chechen
commission monitoring the July 30 military accord.

Meszaros said the OSCE mission today received a
letter from Chairman of the Chechen National Revival
Government Salambek Khadzhiyev to the effect that the
government overturned the order of Groznyy’s Mayor
Beslan Gantemirov, who demanded on September 29
that the OSCE mission quit Grozny.

Meszaros described the situation in Chechnya as com-
plex.

He said he was is in dialogue with both the command
of federal troops and the Russian leadership.

With the troublesome situation in place, it is crucial not
to depart from the logic of searching a peaceful solution
to the problem towards the logic of war, Meszaros said.

He expressed the hope that the conflicting parties would
put the work of the Russian-Chechen commission and
the implementation of the July 30 military accord on
the track.

Meszaros neither confirmed nor denied the Chechen
claims that federal troops bombarded the villages of
Rochni-Chu and Mesker-Yurt on Sunday.

He said he did not go to those sites because he was
attending to security of the Groznyy OSCE mission
these days.

Khasbulatov Says Russia Must Arbitrate

AU1010181095 Vienna DIE PRESSE in German
10 Oct 95 pS

{Interview with Ruslan Khasbulatov, mediator in Chech-
nya, by Elke Windisch; place and date not given: "If the
People Want It, I Will Go Into Politics")

[FBIS Translated Text] [Windisch] The Russian press is
already celebrating you as a hope for Chechnya. They

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 53

are recommending Yeltsin to let Khasbulatov do as he
pleases. Does he allow you that?

[Khasbulatov] The objective of my trip to Chechnya
was the continuation of my peace mission last autumn
— before the invasion of Russian troops — where I had
already tried to achieve a consensus between Dudayev
and his opponents, without taking sides with either of
them. I have not done anything else now, but this
time with the approval of the Kremlin. This week I
will summarize the results in a memorandum for Boris
Yeltsin.

[Windisch] Experts assess Yeltsin's about-face regard-
ing Khasbulatov rather as an act of despair. Is the Krem-
lin at its wit’s end regarding Chechnya?

[Khasbulatov] The cardinal mistake in Chechnya is that
Yeltsin is trying to do the splits there — to create
peace and, at the same time, to back the politicians
who support Moscow’s power. An absurd situation
has developed. Russia is negotiating with the Dudayev
government, which is illegitimate according to the
official interpretation and which has refused to tolerate
that the Khachiyev government, which was appointed
by Moscow, participates in these negotiations. I want to
force Russia to finally set priorities in Chechnya.

[Windisch] What do you propose in concrete terms?

(Khasbulatov] From time immemorial, occupiers have
first taken over the power and thus also responsibility
in the territories subjugated by them. Precisely that is
also contained in my memorandum. Until the elections,
Russia must take over on a trust basis and, as an
arbiter, be above the parties to the conflict. And it
must immediately start the reconstruction work. In this
way, Russia could create the basis in Chechnya that it
currently does not have.

[Windisch] However, the majority of the Chechens
wants the right to independence.

{Khasbulatov] Sovereignty cannot be achieved at once,
first concessions are important as a gesture of good will.
I have proposed that the taxes on oil exports be remitted
for Chechnya in the next five years.

{Windisch} In order to push that through, you would
have to go back into politics.

{Khasbulatov] If my compatriots want it, I will accept.
However, it is still a long way to that because there are
enough influential forces in Russia that want to prevent
it with all their might.

{Windisch} Do you mean Yeltsin? More than once, you
have called him your mortal enemy.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

54 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

(Khasbulatov] At the moment, the Chechen crisis makes
me his ally against my will. Russia’s incompetence in
solving the Chechen crisis is above all due to the fact
that political and strategic interests of important groups
within the leadership are no longer in agreement. Yeltsin
himself is increasingly becoming inconvenient for the
reactionary forces that are banking on a new war.

‘Emergency’ State Seen as Useless

MK1010153495 Moscow MOSKOVSKIY
KOMSOMOLETS in Russian 10 Oct 95 pp 1, 4

{Commentary by Yuliya Kalinina: "State of Emergency
in Groznyy Is Already in Place, But This Does Not
Impede Terrorists]

{[FBIS Translated Text] Now in the wake of the assas-
sination attempt on Romanov, the Russian authorities
have been confronted with the question of what to do
about Chechnya next — to impose a state of emergency
or not to; will it help guarantee order and the safety of
citizens or not? On Monday [9 October], the president
and the premier discussed this problem with experts.
They are supposed to exchange opinions in this regard
when they meet on Tuesday.

In the meantime, experts’ opinions are divided. General
Kulikov, internal affairs minister, thinks it is necessary
to impose a state of emergency; his deputy believes a
state of emergency should be imposed only in Groznyy
and Achkhoy-Martan Rayon where the crime situation is
the worst, whereas the president's plenipotentiary Oleg
Lobov speaks against the imposition of extraordinary
measures.

The law "On the Legal Regime of the State of Emer-
gency” calls for the following measures: a special entry
and exit regime; restrictions on the freedom of move-
ment; enhanced protection of public order; a ban on
gatherings, meetings, demonstrations and strikes; re-
strictions on the movement of vehicles, and searches
thereof. Additionally imposed could be a curfew; re-
strictions on freedom of the press; examination of pa-
pers in crowded places; and examination of personal
possessions and homes. The operation of political par-
ties and movements could be suspended, and sales of
arms and alcohol could be restricted.

All these things are already in place in Chechnya,
if only with the exception of the ban on meetings,
the suspension of the operation of political parties,
and restrictions of the sale of arms and alcohol. Gen.
Kulikov evidently thinks that if parties, meetings, and
alcohol are banned, this will make the internal troops’
task in imposing law and order much easier.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

In the meantime, the Russian special services working
in Chechnya have had too many flops lately.

The tunnel where the terrorist act took place was
not guarded despite its “strategic” significance. It was
unguarded because it was difficult to ensure the safety
of sentries. Roadblocks are always set up in the open so
that no one can steal up to them in the dark undetected,
and setting up such a reliable roadblock in the tunnel
(or near it) was impossible.

For some reason, Romanov was moving about Groznyy
in a jeep, although what he indeed needed was an armor-
plated Mercedes.

There are no results of special services’ work in
evidence. Their latest achievement is a Basayev mirage
appearing in Dagestan. At the same time, "Budennovsk"
terrorists routinely come to Groznyy and nobody cares.

Either our special services are cool as a cucumber or,
like the interior troops units, they are totally helpless in
Chechnya. A state of emergency will hardly help them
in either case.

On the other hand, if no state of emergency is imposed
now, Gen. Kulikov will come out unscathed in any
situation. Whatever happens, whatever further terrorist
act takes place, the commander’s responsibility will be
minimal. It was he who insisted on imposing a state of
emergency. They did not hear — what can he do now?
Making his decision, the president evidently will have
to take account of this tricky thing to the detriment of
the position of Oleg Lobov, who has already had some
experience of the Chechnya reality.

Anyone with similar experience cannot help realizing
that no state of emergency can rectify the situation
and ease the work of the interior troops’ units; on the
contrary, it will cause yet another outburst of anger
and, correspondingly, more provocations, bombings,
and shots from behind the corner, which will have to
be answered by the military. Thus, the goal pursued
by the criminals who made the assassination attempt
on Gen. Romanov will be solved: The peace process
will be comfortably derailed; the elections will have to
be postponed; and the present state of “neither war nor
peace” will drag out indefinitely.

‘Emergency’ State Seen as Foregone Conclusion

MK1010141495 Moscow KOMMERSANT-DAILY
in Russian 10 Oct 95 p]

{Report by Georgiy Bovt and Dmitriy Kamyshev: "The
Worse, the Better. The Only Question Is for Whom")

([FBIS Translated Text] According to his press service,
Boris Yeltsin spent the greater part of the past weekend

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

and of yesterday thinking about the possibility of
introducing a state of emergency in Groznyy and
consulting his aides on the issue. Even so, according
to well-informed sources, within the next few hours no
decision is likely to be made: The Russian leadership
does not want to “act rashly." However, given that
the “power” structures and members of Chechnya’s
pro-Moscow government have already supported this
measure in no uncertain terms, an edict on a state
of emergency will probably appear sooner or later
— unless it transpires that the attempt on General
Romanov’s life was the work of "friends of Russia."
Thus, for a second time in the past year in Chechnya,
Moscow is facing a Rubicon separating a "poor peace"
from a “good war.”

The delay over the decision to impose a state of emer-
gency may be due to the fact that Yeltsin has decided to
hedge things by forming a certain collective opinion, al-
though, according to his aide Leonid Smirnyagin, there
is nO consensus among the presidential analysts: The
correlation between those who believe that this move
is expedient and those opposed to it is approximately
3:2. True, people whose opinion Yeltsin heeds above
all spoke up promptly. The tone was set by the gov-
ernment, saying on Saturday [7 September] that it con-
siders it necessary to introduce a state of emergency
in Groznyy and ordering the federal forces command
to take “all measures with respect to illegal armed for-
mations destabilizing the situation in Chechnya.” De-
fense Minister Grachev also sent word all the way down
from Geneva, proposing that negotiations with the Du-
dayevites be stopped.

Internal Affairs Minister Anatoliy Kulikov, in an inter-
view with ORT, said approximately the same thing, al-
though not with such a soldier's straightforwardness. He
accused the Dudayevites of scuttling the military agree-
ments and attempting, before the elections, to change the
balance of forces in their favor by force. The "power"
people were also supported by Nationalities Minister
Vyacheslav Mikhaylov, who said that the introduction
of a state of emergency not only will not harm the peace
settlement, but will in fact “create a basis for address-
ing many problems.” Mikhaylov’s deputy in the Russian
delegation, Arkadiy Volskiy, explained that the state of
emergency has long been in force de facto and that it
should have been given a de jure basis immediately af-
ter federal troops were brought into Chechnya.

Apparently in order to facilitate the president’s choice,
direct participants in the events have joined the “emer-
gency agitation” drive. The first agitation event was the
Saturday grenade-launcher attack on the OSCE mis-
sion in Groznyy. Then came a report about a four-
hour nighttime fight for the Russian troops’ headquarters

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 55

in Khankal, considerately blown up by official "mili-
tary sources" from "routine firing” on a Russian check-
point. The latest information salvo came yesterday from
members of the National Salvation Committee and the
Chechnya national revival government, who supported
the idea of introducing a state of emergency not only
in Groznyy but also in Achkhoy-Martan Rayon, where
“the emerging crime situation is the worst." Against the
backdrop of such a harmonious chorus of the advocates
of a “state of emergency," the president's silence ap-

pears stoic.

What is bothering Boris Yeltsin? Why is he hesitating
in such a clear-cut situation? Apparently his inborn
intuition tells him that in a situation when so many
are persistently prompting him to introduce a state of
emergency, he should look very carefully indeed before
leaping. After all, the attack on Romanov followed
the same pattern as the attack on Lobov. It looks too
much like a provocation. The classic question arises:
Who could be acting according to the “the worse,
the better" principle? The Dudayevites? Yes. Yet they
are far from unanimous: There are intransigents like
Basayev and there are those with whom Romanov has
come to terms more than once, like Maskhadov. The
intransigents could stand to gain from a scuttled peace
process. But in the present day “neither war nor peace"
situation the implacable have enjoyed a number of
obvious advantages: They have been creeping back to
power. And they did not really need to provoke the
Russian military monster into a showdown. Noieworthy
is the position of the "pro-Moscow opposition:" They
might be discontented with the sluggish negotiating
process: The final word in the emerging so-called self-
defense detachments would, in all likelihood, belong not
to Khadzhiyev, Gantemirov, Labazanov, and others of
their ilk, but to the Dudayevites.

Could there have been interest in the assassination of
a high-ranking Russian official as a cause for resuming
the war on the "Russian side of the barricade"? After
all, it cannot be ruled out that the attack on Lobov could
have been inspired by those who did not like the way he
has directed the money flow going for the restoration of
Chechnya. There are too many of those seeking to cling
to this "life-giving stream,” which is almost impossible
to monitor. Then it should be assumed that the attack
on Romanov was a provocation so as, in the ensuing
bloody mess, to replay the game called "the restoration
of Chechnya." Very many stand to gain from the war
in Chechnya. And there is something for the president
to think about.

Reportage on Premier Chernomyrdin’s Visit to
Bashkiria

Promises No Rise in Energy Prices This Year

LD1110110595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1047 GMT 11 Oct 95

[By Gennadiy Yezhov, Raul Tukhvatullin]}

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] UFA, October 11 (ITAR-
TASS) — The Russian government will retain the
current prices for transport, electricity and gas till
the beginning of next year, Russian Premier Viktor
Chernomyrdin told a meeting of the heads of the
city and regional administrations and enterprises of
Bashkiria on Wednesday.

The Russian Autonomous Republic of Bashkiria is
suffering from a serious shortage of gas, though several
main gas pipe lines run through the republic. Gas
enterprises have cut off the supplies of the fuel both
for Bashkiria’s industry and its essential needs because
the republic is in debt for the gas. The prices for the
fuel are continuing to rise in the republic. Bashkiria
has to use fuel oil, thus yet more deteriorating the
unfavourable ecological situation. This causes protests
among the four-million population of the republic.

Chernomyrdin instructed the leadership of the gas sector
to take urgent measures to rectify the situation in
Bashkiria. The premier proposed resuming stable gas
supplies to the republic and settling the debt by writing
off mutual non-payments.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

Sees Power-Sharing as Expanding Sovereignty

LD1010192295 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1806 GMT 10 Oct 95

{By Raul Tukhvatullin and Gennadiy Yezhov]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] UFA, October 10 (ITAR-
TASS) — The Russian government is firmly against
any plans of splitting Russia into governorships, that
is against turning ethnic formations into administrative
districts. "We are for federalism in our country,” Russian
Prime Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin said on Tuesday in
Ufa at the celebrations devoted to the fifth anniversary
of the passing of the declaration on Bashkiria’s state
sovereignty within the Russian Federation.

Chernomyrdin said the federal authorities now work out
a plan for nationalities policy of the Russian Federation.
The drafting of the document is supervised by President
Boris Yeltsin. Subjects of the Federation are involved
in the effort.

In addition, the Russian government is drafting a federal
programme for the development of Turkic peoples and a
federal draft law on national-cultural autonomies which
will help preserve the right of cultural communities
to develop regardless of whefe their members live.
The Russian premier said a nation’s right to self-
determination is recognised by the federal authorities in
the country with taking into account specific conditions,
but still with preserving united Russia.

Chernomyrdin conveyed to the participants in the cel-
ebration meeting in Ufa the congratulatory message of
Russian President Boris Yeltsin on their national day,
the Day of the Republic, October 11.

This date is an important milestone in the establish-
ment of new federal relations in Russia, the presidential
message says. Chernomyrdin recalled that in the 16th
century Bashkiria voluntarily joined Russia. The events
of the past five years showed again that our peoples
should live side-by-side, he said, reading out the presi-
dential message.

A number of matters of fundamental importance re-
garding the destiny of the unified Russian federal state
were resolved in the five years since the proclama-
tion of Bashkirian sovereignty, Chernomyrdin said. Rus-
sia’s federal structure has passed the test for durability.
This was largely promoted by the signing by the Rus-
sian federal authorities and Bashkiria of the package of
documents on power-sharing. This actually expanded
Bashkiria’s sovereignty.

Similar documents were signed with Tatarstan, with
the Sakha Republic (Yakutia), with Kabardino-Balkaria.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

Chernomyrdin said that the federal authorities, specif-
ically the government, are now engaged in vast work
with other regions of the Russian Federation to sign
similar documents with them.

Regarding the state of affairs in the Bashkirian econ-
omy, Chernomyrdin pointed to the stabilisation in the
area, which is promoted by lawmaking of local author-
ities.

They have passed of late more than 150 documents
regarding local legislation, also economic. On the other
hand, the package of documents on power-sharing
expanded the rights of the local authorities in property,
granting tax privileges and conducting the local tax
policy, as well as the policy to attract investment into
the republic’s economy.

The only thing that Bashkiria lacks now is the presence
of qualified experts in the market economy, the Russian
premier said. He suggested that a department of man-
agers be open at a local high educational establishment
and that Bashkirian representatives be trained in man-
agement in Moscow.

Chernomyrdin will hold a meeting with heads of
Bashkirian cities and districts on Wednesday. The im-
plementation of the federal programme of Bashkiria’s
development will be one of the main items to be
discussed. Then the prime minister plans to fly to
Moscow.

Local CPRF Leader Interviewed on Volgograd
Elections

MM1010145995 Moscow PRAVDA in Russian
7 Oct 95 pp 1,2

{Interview with State Duma Deputy Alevtina Vik-
torovna Aparina, first secretary of the Volgograd CPRF
Oblast Committee, by Valentina Nikiforova, under the
“Events and Comments” rubric; place and date not
given: “Stalingrad Breakthrough" — first two para-
graphs are introduction]

{[FBIS Translated Text] So we now know that at the
elections to the Volgograd City Duma candidates from
the oblast CPRF [Communist Party of the Russian
Federation) organization won 22 of the 24 deputies’
seats.

Evidently people thought hard before the present elec-
tions to the city Duma. To the Communists’ credit,
they were able to divert the people from rally verbiage
about human rights toward specific implementation of
these rights. State Duma Deputy Alevtina Viktorovna
Aparina, first secretary of the Volgograd CPRF Obkom
[Oblast Committee], spoke about this.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 57

{Aparina] Before we had even had time to count up,
I heard it on the Russian Television Channel: The
Communists have come to power in Volgograd! They
account for 80 percent of the seats. That's not bad,
I thought. But, knowing their uncertain arithmetic, I
checked their statistics just in case. It turned out that the
figure was not 80 percent, but 92 percent. And another
amendment: not Communist deputies, but CPRF oblast
organization members. Some of our deputies do not
belong to the party.

But that is not the main thing. The deputies include
specialists, production organizers, and administrators.
The future of the million-strong city will be determined
by two directors of major enterprises — “Medtekhnika"
and a shipbuilding company, heads of workshops at the
tractor plant and the “Barrikady” plant, and engineers
from the “Avrora” motor plant. There are also teachers
and representatives of commercial structures, as well as
former servicemen.

Our candidates won in an honest struggle. This was not
easy. But tell me, was it easy for L. Rokhlin, hero of
the Chechen war? He has an armed personnel carrier, a
guard of honor company, and a whole army of officers
and men. They distributed packages around apartments
day and night. And they arrived at the “Barrikady” plant
in the armed personnel carrier — they threw everyone
into a panic: It is a defense plant.

They overdid it. The people, yearning for order and a
firm hand, had a good look at this hand in front of their
own faces for 100 days before the elections, and did
not vote for it. Did people become frightened? Did they
see a threat of a military dictatorship? In my opinion,
no. They realized that the military will defend their own
corporate interests. L. Rokhlin’s participation in Cher-
nomyrdin’s “Home” in particular generated concern. On
the day of the elections, people said to me: “Let the
Army stick to its own matters.” And not a single can-
didate from the military got through, although no doubt
there were some worthy candidates.

The mass media are now attributing our success to the
fact that “the Communists did not have any decent ri-
vals.” This is not true. Several heads of rayon adminis-
trations and chiefs of police precincts took part in the
elections (they were very active in seeking power, as it
happens), as well as public figures and leaders of major
organizations.

The competition was not easy. But we created an elec-
tion headquarters, and Nikolay Ivanovich Bogomolov
became its chief. The public university of socialism,
democracy, and human rights, formed on the initiative
of Obkom Secretary Lidiya Vasilyevna Vorobyeva, took
an active part -— it involves over 50 students, mainly

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

58 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

young people. We are very grateful to our public assis-
tants: They went around apartments, handed out leaflets
and newspapers, and spoke to people. They wrote down,
remembered, and passed on their instructions and re-
quests to the analytical group at the obkom.

Another group spent some time at enterprises. I per-
sonally met with people at 20 enterprises. This is what
we would do: We would go to four or five plants in
a rayon in a day, and then in the evening we would
conduct rayon meetings with the electorate. And what
I noticed was that young people were keen to come to
the meetings. Maybe because they were being person-
ally addressed. Or maybe the fact that their peers were
taking part in our team interested them.

As a result, people voted for our candidates. A funny
thing happened. In one okrug we did not put forward a
candidate. People arriving at the polling place were ask-
ing for the ballot papers “naming Chekhov (candidate
for the city hall whom we supported) and the Commu-
nists." And when they did not obtain any, they left —
without voting.

We may rejoice at our success, and this will do us no
harm. But we understand the difficult position we have
found ourselves in: A Communist Duma under an anti-
Communist regime. Some people are already predicting
that the duma will only be involved in politics.

Nothing of the sort! We understand how great our
responsibility is. That is why the day after the elections
we held a sitting of the Obkom Secretariat, and stated
officially that we are assuming responsibility for the
deputies’ work. We have created an organizational
committee to prepare the first session. Our analytical
center has prepared proposals for the work schedule.
We have reached agreement with Yuriy Chekhov, who
has been elected mayor, that we will hold a city
assembly for the electorate in the near future, and invite
bosses of enterprises and municipal services to it so
that we can discuss and organize a schedule together.
Obviously providing the Volgograd inhabitants with
food, preparing for the winter, and creating the right
conditions for the local commodity producers will be
the main problems.

Of course, this is an excessive burden for 24 deputies.
Therefore we would like to suggest that specialists
should also join the sectoral commissions in addition to
the deputies, — that is, we will expand the number of
people answerable for life in the city. Incidentally, it has
been decided to teach the deputies and their assistants
twice a week. It is possible that the proposal of the
Volgograd inhabitants to name their elected organ a city
soviet rather than a duma will thus be realized. We are
not here to dictate, but to create.

Novosibirsk Passes Decisions To Elect New

Economic Issues

Duma Budget Chairman Zadornov on 1996 Draft
Budget

MM1110109395 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 7 Oct 95 pp 1, 3

["First Person" stenographic account by A. Yevgenev
of meeting between Mikhail Zadornov, chairman of the
Russian State Duma Committee for the Budget, Taxes,
Banks, and Finance, and ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
Editorial Office journalists chaired by Chief Editor A.
Yurkov; place and date of meeting not given: “Mikhail
Zadornov: ‘1996 Budget Will Not Clear Duma Hurdle”
— first paragraph is ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA intro-
duction]

{FBIS Translated Text] When Mikhail Zadornov was
elected to the State Duma he could scarcely have
believed that he would end up in the hottest seat
— the post of chairman of the Committee for the
Budget, Taxes, Banks, and Finance. How crucial the
budget is — all the contradictions and incredible tension
of the transitional period are encapsulated in it. The
1996 budget is no exception. The Federation Council
has already announced the formation of a conciliation
commission. Most likely the same will happen in the
Duma. That means that hard, painstaking work to find
agreement lies ahead.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] Mikhail Mikhaylovich,
while most State Duma members were making the
most of their carefree summer vacation the budget

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

committee’s work was well under way. The main
financial document of next year — the federal budget
— was being studied. First of all I would like to know
how the current draft differs from the preceding ones?
What are its special features?

[Zadornov] There are few differences. The working
group of the Committee for the Budget, Taxes, Banks,
and Finance, which, incidentally, includes representa-
tives of various factions, reached at least two conclu-
sions at the very fist session. The budget presented by
the government is essentially a continuation of the pre-
vious economic policy. That is the first thing. Second,
in all probability the Duma will not be able to agree to
this draft.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] But the government did
not promise a change of economic policy.

{Zadornov] Look, any budget is formulated on the
basis of a forecast of the country’s socioeconomic
development. The forecast for 1996 incorporates the
inflation indicator of 1.2 percent per month. We consider
it to be absolutely unrealistic. Let me remind you that
a year ago we were originally promised | percent by
December, but during debates with the government we
got the average to 3 percent.

What do we have today? In the first half-year the aver-
age monthly inflation was 10 percent and throughout the
year as a whole it will at best be 7-8 percent. What has
this led to? The defense order, fuel to be transported to
the North, budget-funded employees’ wages — every-
thing has been calculated on the basis of this overop-
timistic forecast. Consequently we have fallen behind
with indexing. The population’s real monetary incomes
have decreased by 10 percent over seven months. The
same defense order is twice as expensive as envisaged
by the budget. It has been placed but there is nothing
with which to pay for it. It is the same in any other
sphere.

If you look, all the main items of the budget have ap-
parently been funded, but everywhere there is insuffi-
cient money — because of accelerated inflation it has
lost one-third of its value. This situation cannot be re-
peated next year, that would be fraught with social con-
sequences that are too grave. Especially as the toughness
of financial policy has an election air about it. It is being
applied above all to the defense industry, health care,
education, science, and investment.

At the same time there are sectors which are not paying
full taxes — because of nonpayments ~ some other
reason -—— but they are having quite a reasonable life.
The shortfalls have already reached 28 trillion rubles
[R], which is mainly attributable to a clearly defined

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 59

range of self-managing economic components — the
oil and gas industries, metallurgical enterprises, and
railroad transport. ‘*wer one-half of underpayments are
associated with the @rtivity of 46 enterprises. No serious
sanctions are being applied to them, they are not going
bankrupt. So this is the kind of unhealthy polarity
that has developed. On the one hand budget-funded
employees have been squeezed extremely hard, and
on the other hand there is a group of untouchables
inhabiting a completely different dimension. In these
conditions the government is finding it possible, without
bothering with explanations, to give tax deferments and
budget loans to the chosen few.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] But surely strategic,
promising areas must be supported somehow. Current
taxes are crippling everybody.

[Zadornov] Taxes are another thing causing objections
to the budget. You know that in spring the State
Duma passed a law on the principles of a tax system.
According to our estimates it eases the tax burden
by approximately 10 percent of an enterprise’s gross
income, reduces the number of actual taxes, and changes
the relationship between the taxpayer and the tax organs.
But the government, through the Federation Council,
blocked it. What is being offered in its place? In place
of the abolished tax on the above-norm wages fund,
what is being introduced is not even a tax but a system
of collecting fines for letting wage increases outstrip
inflation. It is bad enough that nobody could explain to
us how such a mechanism could work in reality. In our
view it is also inherently flawed in that it will precipitate
a reduction in production volumes and force enterprises
to get out of debt by hook or by crook. Or there is this
scenario: The special tax is abolished but at the same
time value-added tax is increased by 1 percent. Where
is the logic here?

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] Does it not seem to you
that we are going round in circles on the tax question?
There are several schemes for a tax structure. In 1992
we chose one of them, an entirely civilized one, and we
are currently following it. Yet we cannot collect taxes
in full, they are slipping through our hands in various
ways. In that case is it not better to change the model
— to levy taxes on what cannot be hidden: resources
used in production, land, real estate, and so on?

[Zadornov] I would like to remind you that it took seven
to eight months to adapt to the current tax system. It was
only after that that we started to collect something. So
switching from one tax system to another is in itself an
extremely painful act.

But in principie the process is moving in the direction
you mentioned. Taxes on enterprise property, payments

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

60 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

for natural resources, and payments for water, land, and
SO on are increasing. But let us look at things soberly:
If we force a plant to pay three to five times more
for its resources, will it be able to do this given the
current financial situation? Do we stand to gain much?
At the end of the day there is such a concept as a tax
base. If in three years your production has halved, what
are you in a position to collect? You can reduce the
overall level, simplify the tax system, and stimulate an
increase in production. Then it cannot be ruled out that
in two to three years we will receive some increase in
tax revenues.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] What about the budget’s
expenditure element — what is new here?

[Zadornov] Funding of all spheres is being cut in
real terms. The proportion of expenditure is effectively
increasing for only three items — international activity,
servicing the foreign and domestic debt, and expenditure
on law enforcement organs. We cannot agree with this
approach either. Especially as there lie behind this things
which are not sufficiently explained. For example, the
draft budget envisages an overall increase in the power
structures’ establishments. An extra 100,000 people are
due to appear next year within the MVD [Ministry of
Internal Affairs] system alone.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] But this step can be
viewed as a natural reply to society’s demand to
intensify the fight against crime.

{Zadornov] Any task can be tackled in two ways —
either with brawn or with brain. Our MVD’s current
establishment level exceeds the establishments of the
internal affairs organs of the former Soviet Union —
that is, of countries with a population twice that of
Russia’s current population. This excludes the municipal
police forces, which are funded from the local budget in
Moscow and other cities. But you can see for yourself
that quantity is not always translated into quality.

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] Mikhail Mikhaylovich,
what about the law on veterans? It is a scandal that
this important law, which affects the interests of the
most socially vulnerable section of society, has not been
backed up with funding.

[Zadornov] Some R6.5 trillion have been allocated
for the law on veterans. This will be directly funded
through local budgets. Provision has been made for
this money to be moved as transfers from the federal
budget to local level. Everything would seem to be in
order. But when we started to dig a bit deeper certain
unpleasant aspects surfaced. For example, aside from
the Federation components we have so-called closed
territoriai-administrative formations — approximately

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

30 cities belonging to the Defense Ministry, Atomic
Energy Ministry, etc. They are wholly funded from
federal budget funds. We started looking at the budget
but there was no allusion to “veterans.” We have now
requested all materials on the law: If this is how matters
stand we will rec’cck everything anew.

I am not inclined to absolve either parliament or the
committee of responsibility for the current situation. But
when our committee considers the draft of some law we
always demand financial and economic substantiation.
Unfortunately this has not become the rule for the
Duma as a whole. Sometimes when there is the slightest
objection the question is put as follows: Are you for
Or against veterans, for or against solving the nuclear
energy problem? But lobbyists are not worried that there
are no funds for the law and that because of this it will
remain an empty declaration....

[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] Mikhail Mikhaylovich,
your committee is also elaborating banking legislation.
Right now, when commercial banks are “collapsing” and
delaying payments to their clients, depositors are feeling
very uncomfortable. What is being done to protect their
rights?

[Zadornov} We have adopted several laws — on the
Central Bank, on banks and banking activity and amend-
ments to it, on the securities market. What is the most
important thing here? Privatization of the Savings Bank
has been prohibited. As the most popular bank it remains
under the state's patronage, and that means it remains
the most reliable bank. So that, in the event of bank-
ruptcy, a bank which has “gone to the wall” can always
pay its depositors, it has been stipulated that the sum
of citizens’ funds attracted by a bank cannot be greater
than its own funds. In addition, a kind of probationary
period has been established for new banks: They can
Start working with the population only after they have
been open for two years. There are also many interest-
ing aspects to the law on the securities market, which,
incidentally, does not allow finance companies to work
with the population without the consent of the Securi-
ties Commission. But the president has vetoed this law
as well as the law on banks and banking activity. And
to this day they have not been improved one iota. All
because some people think that the status of their organ
as defined by the law does not correspond to how they
want to see it. In short, bureaucrats’ personal ambitions
have gotten in the way.

{[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] The following procedure
exists in Finland: If a bank goes bankrupt the investor
not only gets back the hard-earned money which he
deposited there but also all the interest due to him.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

[Zadornov] We adopted the Law on the Compulsory
Insurance of the Population’s Deposits in its first
reading. Out of admission and regular dues the banks
themselves will form a so-called compulsory insurance
fund. In the event of bankruptcy a client is guaranteed
to recover 90 percent of the sum invested.

{[ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA] The current State Duma
has adopted around 200 laws. The president has signed

Government Stance on 1996 Budget Viewed

MM1010134695 Moscow IZVESTIYA (Financial
Section) in Russian 10 Oct 95 pl

[Report by Yevgeniya Pismennaya under general head-
ing: “Government May Agree To Concede | Percent
Inflation to Parliament"}

{[FBIS Translated Text] Three positions set forth in
the draft budget for next year will be defended by
the government: the pace of inflation, the level of the
budget deficit, and the noninflationary nature of sources
of financing the budget deficit (that is the government
does not agree to Central Bank credits).

Vladimir Petrov, first deputy minister of finances, claims
that all government policy depends on these com-
ponents. To reject them means failing to fulfill the
medium-term programs and coordinated statements with
the Central Bank. Moreover, talks with international fi-
nancial organizations on credits and extinguishing them
and on restructuring the foreign debt will be consider-
ably complicated.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 61

On the rest the government agrees to compromises. For
instance, interbudget relations can be widely discussed
as can the structure of budget expenditure or several
questions of taxation policy.

Before submitting the draft budget to the State Duma the
government calculated it in seven options, including one
with an inflation rate of 2.5-3 percent and 5 percent a
month. The executive branch believes that in a situation
where the rate of inflation rises to 5 percent production
stagnation and a substantial rise in unemployment are
inevitable. Inflation rates reaching 3 percent presuppose
less depressing indicators but nonetheless ones which
do not lead to positive results. It became clear from
both calculations that the government will be unable
to index wages at the same rate at which inflation is
growing. Actually this had already been demonstrated
graphically enough in previous years. If inflation rates
are kept down to 1 percent the indexing of wages will
even be somewhat higher than inflation.

In the opinion of Vladimir Petrov achieving an inflation
rate of 1-1.2 percent a month is feasible but on condition
that “a number of key measures" are taken to put the
price formation of some base industries in order. Obvi-
ously a tougher prices policy is planned. As an example
of these measures an interdepartmental organ — the en-
ergy commission — will be created. It should engage
in implementing prices policy in the electricity indus-
try at federal level which, the government suggests, will
lead to a considerable reduction of inflation rates. It is
planned to do the same with rail transport and other ba-
sic sectors.

Vladimir Petrov assures us that the government knows
the mechanism for implementing these programs. As
early as the fourth quarter of 1995 the complex of these
measures will be put into operation at full capacity.

LD1010214695 Moscow INTERFAX in English

[FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 10 (INTER-

Board of Statistics has said. In the same period last year
the GDP was down 12%.

Last month Russia’s GDP remained unchanged as
compared wiih the previous month and fell by 1% as
compared with September last year. Russia produced
2% less electricity than in January through September
1994 and the production of coal was down 4%, of gas,
2%, and of oil, 3% than in the same period last year.

Compensation Mechanism for Savings Worked Out

LD1010211595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
2046 GMT 10 Oct 95

[By Liliya Kuznetsova]

(FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, October 10
(ITAR-TASS) — The Commission of the Public
Chamber at the Russian President on socio-economic
problems has considered and endorsed the mechanism
of the compensation for Russian citizens’ savings
devalued during the reform.

Savings deposits that existed prior to the reform are
treated in the law "On the Restoration and Protection of
Russian Citizens’ Savings” as the state’s internal debt,
it was said at the commission’s meeting on Tuesday.
What with the budget deficit, there is no real opportunity
for compensating for citizens’ lost savings at present.
Therefore, monetary compensation is envisaged only for
the less protected sections of the population.

Purpose-oriented debt obligations will be used as a
means of payment for privatisation by the mass of the
population. This means that part of property will be
set aside for privatisation not for funds but for debt
obligations.

So any Russian citizen will be able to receive compen-
sation for his or her savings either through purchasing
State property for debt obligations or through entrust-
ing debt obligations to the management of commercial
structures. They are issued with special licences for the
management of state property allocated for the compen-
sation for the lost savings. Commercial structures buy
debt obligations from holders and are entrusted with
some or other facilities of state property as a result.

And there is one more way of compensation when the
Savings remain on a Citizen’s noncash account in the
Savings Bank. Holders of debt obligations receive the
interest that the state can give in a given economic
Situation. Their savings will be fully compensated for
in the long run.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

New Bonds’ Impact on Gold Sales, Output
Examined

MMI1010132395 Moscow IZVESTIYA (Financial
Section) in Russian 6 Oct 95 p 3

[Article by Elmar Guseynov: "Gold Reserves Might Not
Be Enough To Guarantee Federal Loans"]

(FBIS Translated Text] The year 1996 could be the year
when a full-fledged gold market is created in Russia as
well as a period of an unprecedented reduction in the
precious metal’s production in our country.

The development of a gold market will be determined
by the implementation of the 5 September edict signed
by the Russian president, in accordance with which the
issue of federal loan bonds guaranteed by gold is due to
begin. The bonds will be income bonds — the interest
on them will be determined in accordance with LIBOR
{London Interbank Offered Rate] index indicators. The
bonds’ owners, including individual persons, will be
able to exchange the securities for gold freely.

The total amount of gold being alle ated to guarantee
bonds is 30 tonnes. In terms of value, taking account of
the world price of gold, this is around 1.6-1.7 trillion
rubles [R]. The Finance Ministry and Central Bank have
been given the job of devising a mechanism to issue and
sell bonds and provide a normative base for this process.

Roskomdragmet [Russian Federation Committee for
Precious Metals and Precious Stones], which was one
of the initiators of the issue of a gold loan, hoped that
the bonds would be guaranteed by ingots of between 5
grams [g] and | kg. This would make it possible to give
as wide a circle of private investors as possible access
to the bonds. But judging by information received it will
just be a question of 1-kg ingots.

It is not yet clear where the funds from floating a gold
loan will go. Roskomdragmet believes that this money
must be used primarily to purchase the precious metal
from producers, for which there are insufficient funds
at present. Thereby gold, without moving out of the
State reserves, would help to produce new gold. But it
is not ruled out that the funds from the gold loan will be
“gobbled up” and will disappear into the general budget
hole.

Although it is not yet clear how the many problems as-
sociated with the issue of gold bonds will be solved, it
is obvious that on the whole their appearance will lead
to revolutionary movements on the domestic precious
metals market. Permission for private persons to own
gold ingots will entail a sharp widening of the spectrum
and volumes of banks’ security [zalogovyy] transactions
and purchasing transactions involving gold, will initiate

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-196
11 October 1995

a considerable increase in the network of private pur-
chasing centers and pawnshops, and will create a new
and fairly powerful capitals market. Giving nonresidents
permission to buy bonds must inevitably lead to the lib-
eralization of regulations governing the export and im-
port of gold ingots, including for individual persons.

The main obstacle to the normal functioning of a gold-
backed securities market could be the retention of a
foreign exchange band in its present form. At the
moment almost all gold settlements in our country are
in one way or another indexed in dollars. Given the
dollar’s effective devaluation in Russia, this will lead
to a situation whereby the owners of gold bonds will
begin to incur losses and will try to exchange them for
the physical metal as quickly as possible.

It is the foreign exchange band’s introduction that
has already effectively brought Russia’s gold-extracting
industry to its knees. The cost of the metal bought by the
State from producers is tied to the dollar price of gold
on world markets. Consequently, while in the first half
of this year extraction enterprises were paid R63,000 for
1 g of gold, they are now getting only R54,000. In the
same period the cost of raw and other materials used
by gold extractors has increased by approximately 25
percent in ruble prices.

Gold prospectors, for example, have already calculated
that, taking account of ruble inflation, the cost of gold
acquired by the state must now be at least R87,000 just
to preserve the profitability of their production at the
first half-year’s level.

The gold extraction situation also remains unstable
because the state, in irying on the one hand to retain
control over the purchasing of the precious metal, is on
the other hand itself undermining the sector’s sources
of funding. Thus, R10 trillion was incorporated into
the 1995 budget for the acquisition of precious metals
from producers. At the moment around R3 trillion has
been spent on these purposes. But only R214 million
have gone on buying gold from the budget. Around
another R800 billion has been received from the sale
of gold from state reserves and R2 billion from the sale
of Gokhran [State Depository of Valuables] valuables,
mainly uncut diamonds.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 63

One can only guess where the R9.8 trillion incorporated
into the budget for buying gold went — whether
it went on the war in Chechnya or on plugging
other holes in the budget. Meanwhile gold is being
used very actively to cover the government's budget
miscalculations. According to our data around 30 tonnes
of gold from state reserves must be sold in the near
future to cover the state’s debts to pensioners.

Because of the lack of funds a reliable source of
producing secondary gold — buying metal from the
population — is drying out. While the State Central
Purchasing Organization handed over 12 tonnes of the
precious metal in scrap to Gokhran in 1988, it managed
to purchase only 4.1 tonnes in 1994 and no more than
2.5 tonnes in the first half of 1995.

This is at a time when there now are many more people
wishing to sell gold jewelry than in 1988, although the
purchase price is only R37,265 or around $8.20 per
gram of 583-standard scrap. The benefit to the state of
buying gold from the population is obvious, because it
can sell gold to jewelry producers at R1 10,000 per gram.

Maybe allowing all individual and legal persons to trade
freely in gold ingots, reinforced by a relaxation in the
sphere of exporting gold ingots out of the country, will
extend banks’ participation in precious metal transac-
tions. At the moment only around 15 banks have con-
cluded contracts with gold producers to purchase a total
volume of only 5.4 tonnes of the metal, which is no
more than 3.5 percent of annual extraction. Of these
banks the largest purchases are being planned by Bank-
Kredit, Inkombank, Promstroybank, Rossiyskiy Kredit,
the International Bank for Reconstruction and Devel-
opment, Kuzbassotsbank, Bank Yenisey, Lesprombank,
Natsionainyy Kredit, and Imperiyal.

If the foreign exchange band and the methodology for
forming purchase prices are retained in their present
form until the beginning of 1996, an inevitable decrease
in production awaits Russia’s gold extraction. Judging
from data received from individual regions it could total
between 15 and 40 percent of the 1994 ievel.

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

border would be a provocation against our country.

istry has not received an official statement about this.

step, because this would considerably complicate the
situation in Europe."

violate the obligations it assumed in accordance with the
Nuclear Nonproliferation Treaty and will work "to avoid
the geographical proliferation of nuclear weapons.”

Special Envoy Comments on NATO Expansion
Study

AUI1010185095 Kiev HOLOS UKRAYINY

in Ukrainian 5 Oct 95 p6

("Express interview": "NATO’s Forthcoming Expan-
sion"]

(FBIS Translated Text] A special session of the North
Atlantic Cooperation Council was held in Brussels.
It was attended by Volodymyr Vasylenko, envoy for
special missions. Here is what he said:

"A NATO’s study on issues of expanding the alliance
was presented. The objective of the study was to find
out what leads the alliance to such a step and what this
process must be like. I want to stress that the document
did not specify who or when will become a member
of NATO, notably, there is no agreed upon list. It is a
subject for more research."

The document was a culmination of the first stage of
work on resolving the problem of NATO expansion.
It outlines ways for ensuring positive effect of the
process on stability and security in the Euro-Atlantic
Region, commitments imposed by membership of the
alliance, its terms, and criteria. The criteria are, in
particular, as follows: availability of a stable system
of democratic institutions, respect for human rights and
rights of ethnic minorities, absence of ethnic conflicts
or territorial claims upon neighbors, and so on. NATO
Secretary General Willy Claes and other leaders of the
alliance took part in the presentation.

as an effect —- political issues will normalize. Political

Naturally, there are many problems in our relations with
Russia pertaining primarily to the relics of the USSR.
Some Russian politicians, in particular those who are not
in power, issue various statements which do not serve
normalization. Nevertheless, the talks are carried out
on a continuous basis. Three Ukrainian working groups
visited Russia over the last ten days, numerous Russian
delegations visited our country. We shall continue the
talks.

our country?

UKRAINE 65

(Marchuk] Contacts intensified not only with Poland,
but also with NATO and many other states. Ukraine, due
to a series of internal problems, had somewhat curbed its
international activities. Now, in line with the directives
of the president, we are developing foreign policies in
all directions — not only to the East and not only to the
West.

Ukraine and Poland are neighbors sharing a common
history, with strong economic ties and huge potential
for cooperation. For many reasons, to no fault of the
Ukrainian authorities, we have decelerated the tempo
of developing our relations. Currently, however, we
are trying to make up for lost time. I have spoken
with Prime Minister Jozef Oleksy and we agreed we
should meet, but not necessarily officially with full
rites. Working talks are needed. I do wish to stress,
however, that our cooperation is not targeted against
other states. In particular, it is not targeted against
Russia. On the contrary, we are interested not only
in improving Ukrainian-Russian relations, but Polish-
Russian as well.

In line with the provisions of our foreign policy,
relations with Poland — our big neighbor — are a
priority, and I consider that normal.

their continued storage is becoming too risky because
in case of unexpected and spontaneous detonation of
even a single explosive device, a simultaneous explosion
involving many land mines, cartridges, and missiles may
occur, and then blown out windows would seem like a
joke by comparison with what may happen.

{FBIS Translated Text] The Supreme Council Commis-

Ukrainian president’s edict on the resubordination of
the National Guard to the president violates state leg-
islation. According to an INTERFAX-UKRAINE cor-
respondent, this decision was adopted at a meeting of
the commission on Tuesday [10 October]. According
to the commission’s conclusions, the president’s edict
contravenes the Ukrainian law "On the National Guard
of Ukraine” according to which direct control over the
National Guard is exercised by the commander in chief
who is appointed by the parliament at the president’s
submission. The commission concluded that the pres-
ident’s edict contravenes the constitutional treaty be-
tween parliament and the president in accordance with
which issues pertaining to defense and state security are
determined by Ukrainian laws. In addition, the constitu-
tional treaty envisions that the president of Ukraine has
the right to issue edicts exclusively within the limits of
his powers.
